ParlaMint-PL_2015-12-02-sejm-03-1.u234	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Customs officers present at the Sejm Gallery and in front of the monitors! Strong service is a strong country. Customs is a uniform service. Polish Customs Service in free Poland has always been a uniform service, starting from its creation after 123 years of partitions, through years of building independence. Customs officers together with the Border Guard were on guard of Polish borders, defending them against the import of dangerous goods for the lives and health of citizens. [[Game in the O.R.]] As far as exports are concerned, they have guarded the national heritage so that it is not illegally exported abroad. [[♪ The bell ♪]] By collecting customs duties and import taxes, they provided income to the Polish state's budget. In 1939 customs officers actively participated in defending Polish borders and Polish property. Many were sent to camps and camps, many gave their lives at the station in defence of their homeland. Border Guard and Customs perform similar functions, often in the same place, except that the Border Guard is responsible for controlling the movement of persons, and the Customs Service is responsible for controlling goods, including those at risk, such as weapons and ammunition - recently 21 RPGs were arrested at the border crossing point with Ukraine - drugs or psychotropic agents, dangerous materials, radioactive materials, dual-use goods, etc. The Customs Service shall also be responsible for the collection of budgetary entitlements. Customs officers are now protecting the borders of the European Union. Customs borders within the European Union do not exist, but criminal activity continues there, but is more difficult to detect, e.g. transport of waste, drugs, excise goods. And here our activity is typical of police: like police, customs officers have the right to stop, to search premises, to participate in operations of other services, e.g. in the event of a threat to the integrity of the border and in other cases, customs officers may use firearms. Customs investigates, investigates, brings criminal charges to the courts, and they have the right to punish themselves. Customs also fulfills many other tasks important to the security of citizens, and a fraction of what we do can be seen, including tomorrow, in display cases that have been set up in the Sejm since yesterday. We cordially invite you. Customs are serving side by side with border guards, police and other Polish services and services from other countries, in Poland and on foreign missions. Customs officers do not understand the reasons for their discrimination so far. In the service of a similar nature, subjecting themselves to the same regime, exposed to similar threats, performing tasks as important for Polish security as those performed by officers of other uniforms, should have the right to a police pension, compensation or protection in the event of occupational diseases. I ask the Sejm VIII of the term of office, the High Chamber, to restore the ethos and status of the customs officer enjoyed by this profession during the Second Republic of Poland, acting in the public interest. This bill, which I have the honour of presenting, is definitely bringing us closer to this goal. On behalf of 200,000. I have the honour to present to the High House a bill whose adoption should not give rise to any doubt due to both historical, factual and legal necessity. However, the most important thing is - as mentioned in the explanatory memorandum of the proposal - the adoption of this law in order to improve the effectiveness of the Customs Service's activities and to implement further broad legal changes which will allow for a more effective reduction of the so-called "freedom." the shadow economy, i.e. increasing budgetary revenues, increasing citizens' safety and respecting the principles of healthy competition. Thus this project is only the first step, that is the sine qua non condition, which must be met in order to be able to implement the above assumptions. Customs officers have proposals for a package of solutions in this direction and hope to cooperate with the High Chamber in this area in the public interest. The historical and factual necessity of this Act is due to the obligation to comply with the obligations of the Government of the Republic of Poland contained in the Agreement signed on 30 November 1998 between the Government Party and the Union Party, in which the Government of the Republic of Poland at that time was the Government of Prime Minister Jerzy Buzek - committed to establish a uniformed and uniformed Customs Service, whose officers were to have pension rights comparable to other uniformed services. This is an annex - this agreement - to this draft law. The unilateral withdrawal of the Government of the Republic of Poland from the signed agreement means a violation of a number of provisions of the Constitution, such as the Article. 59 (1) (a) 2, Article 7 or Art. 20, since the agreement, I apologize, the 1999 Act did not take into account all the provisions of the agreement. Then there was the position of the government party of 30 January 2008 and a further commitment of the Polish government towards customs officers included in it. In this position, the government has committed itself that the head of the Customs Service will present for discussion a draft modernisation bill aimed at aligning the working conditions of the Customs Service with the working conditions of other uniform services. The position was signed on behalf of the Government by Minister Michał Boni and the then Head of Customs Minister Jacek Dominik. The act containing this solution has never been presented by the head of the Customs Service and has therefore broken once again the government's commitment, another commitment of the Government of the Republic of Poland. There were also promises of the Parliamentary Club Civic Platform submitted here in the plenary room during the adoption of the Customs Service Act in 2009. On behalf of the club, you presented them to Janusz Chwierut, who considered it reasonable to include customs officers in the provision of pension schemes, but putting it back in time. Then in 2011 Prime Minister Donald Tusk at a meeting with officers in Kuźnica Białostocka guaranteed customs officers equal treatment in terms of pensions with officers of other uniformed services. Ladies and gentlemen, the authorities should meet their obligations, otherwise they act against the public interest, also because they simply undermine the principle of the officer's trust in the State. We are now moving on to the need to issue this law legally. Namely, it follows whether it is the result of the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal of 3 March 2015, the signature of the act: K39/13, which has finally confirmed the claims of the customs officers of their legal, unfounded and unfounded discrimination that have been raised for years by the customs authorities in the absence of a pension scheme. I must say with satisfaction that this sentence was also obtained thanks to the involvement of Members of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice who applied to the Constitutional Tribunal on this matter, and their representatives on the day of the application were the current Deputy Minister of Home Affairs, Mr Jarosław Zieliński and President Andrzej Duda, then a Member at the Polish Parliament with whom we spoke and who, as we have seen, perfectly understand the specificities of the administrative relationship, know the rules of operation of the uniformed services and the situation in the Customs Service. It should be pointed out once again that the Customs Service is currently the only uniformed service remaining outside the provisioning pension system. This is the last uniform formation not covered by this system. This illegal injustice must finally be eliminated. The catalogue of uniformed services is closed. There are 11 uniformed services in Poland, and the Supreme Court, the Supreme Administrative Court and the Constitutional Tribunal, of course, also mention the Customs Service, i.e. the only uniformed service outside of the pension system. That's what this discrimination is about. It must not be that only one uniformed service concerns all restrictions on constitutional rights and freedoms, even unknown in other formations, all rigors and restrictions of service, full availability and service dependency, lack of action by the broad-based labour law, and that this is not compensated for the possibility of early retirement, the right to compensation or benefits in the event of an occupational disease. In my judgment, the Constitutional Tribunal held that the differences in the legal situation of Customs officers vis-à-vis other uniformed services were arbitrary and discriminatory. The Court found that the three articles of the Law on pensions of uniformed services were incompatible with the Constitution, indicated that Customs officers have narrower pension rights than officers, e.g. Border Guard or Police despite many common features of these services and that this is unjustified from the point of view of the principle of equality expressed in the Constitution. Thus, the bill which I have the honour to present carries out the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal. The detailed and comprehensive comparativeisation of the status of customs officers vis-à-vis officials of other services is attached to the project. This is a very, very precise report by Mrs. Maria Bwinal, to which I send you very cordially. Thus, the adoption of the proposed laws will enable the State to fulfil its obligation to comply with the abovementioned judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal, but it will also bring the following benefits - here there are 13 points - for the public interest. Firstly, the unification of uniformed services will be completed. Customs officers will be the last of all services to be fully equipped with the same compensation for restrictions and rigours of the service and restrictions on constitutional rights and freedoms resulting from the characteristics of the service relationship. Secondly, there will be an improvement in the efficiency of the Customs Service in its core areas of activity - it is the protection of borders, effective supervision of production, trade in excise products, promotion of entrepreneurship, strengthening the competitiveness of legitimate companies and consequently a significant increase in revenue to the budget. There will be a reduction in the grey economy resulting in a decrease in the profits of organised crime groups, and thus their impact on social and political life in the country. There will be more effective protection of values such as life, health and safety. Thirdly, what will this bill cause? It will strengthen the status of Customs officers carrying out public tasks which are very important for the functioning of the State and citizens. Fourthly, strengthening the security and protection of Customs officials, achieving a balance between requirements, limitations and service conditions and factors that offset the costs of these conditions and restrictions. Fifthly, the introduction of a very important and effective anti-corruption factor in the form of the loss of uniform pension rights for committing a corruption offence by a customs officer. Sixthly, this will stop the process of leaving the service of officials with special qualifications - pension rights are motivated - and will reduce the cost of training their successors. After the seventh, the elimination of pathology in the employment structure will take place together with this law, as the relocation of more posts will be possible according to the needs, as well as the release of normal fluctuations in staff and development opportunities, the possibility of obtaining persons with the desired qualifications for the tasks performed by the Customs Service. Today we have such a pathology that the accepted officers, regardless of their qualifications, will be able to apply for a managerial position no earlier than 40 years from now. That's the pathology we have in Customs right now. The prospect of a higher position is equally far away, so-called. an expert. Which expert will agree to that? What kind of company or institution can afford such a waste of human resources? I'm afraid the Customs will allow it, the superiors. After the eighth, the above-mentioned positive HR processes will increase the efficiency and effectiveness of controls and increase efficiency by only 1% gives around PLN 5 million per year of profit. So every 1% The increase in the effectiveness of the Customs Service will allow for the financing of approximately 150-200 net pensions, without reaching the state budget. After nine years, the employment of young people with appropriate qualifications and an increase in staff fluctuations, as fewer people will carry out the same tasks. The increase in staff fluctuations will allow for a reduction in the number of posts by around 1 000, the employment of these young people in connection with this law, so that it will be possible after leaving, for example. 2,000. hire only $1,000. The resources for this will be sufficient to finance 3.5 thousand. pensions of a minimum of 40% or 1800 pensions of a maximum of 75%. After ten, the entry into force of this law will also result in one-off budgeting of contributions collected in OFE, because that is the principle. After eleven years, the elimination of staff shortages means the elimination of the ongoing costs of delegations, transfers and training for new tasks in the context of the continuous patching of staff shortages. After the twelfth, the allocation costs resulting from transfers of a large number of Customs officers to other places will be eliminated, which in 2015 amounts to more than PLN 4.5 million per year. This will allow the financing of 160.270 pensions, depending on their amount. After the 13th, there will be a strengthening of the customs officer's confidence in the State by fulfilling the government's commitment I mentioned earlier to include customs officers in the provisioning pension system. What is the situation with the Customs Service today? Human resources management has been compromised for 10 years. and now the situation is quite critical. In the Customs Service, more than 30% are people over 50 years of age, while in the Police, Border Guard or Prison Service of such persons is less than 3% That's the comparison. It's a staff from the '90s, so in a few years more than half of it will be in Customs at the age of 50+. It should be further stressed that the amendments proposed by this Act will significantly strengthen and improve the functioning of the Customs Service, while addressing the vast majority of current human and organisational problems, which the Minister of Finance himself confirms. in response to the Parliamentary Interpellation No 10 466 of 19 August 2011. We should ask Dr. Andrzej Halicki, if we agree as a society to ensure that an advanced officer, i.e. a person with reduced psychophysical fitness and lower cognitive abilities, protects us from the influx into Poland of contaminated spirits, toxic waste, weapons, ammunition, psychotropic substances and other materials that pose a real threat to the safety of citizens? A negative response requires concrete action, including the inclusion of officers in the pension scheme for officers of other uniformed services. Ladies and gentlemen! Over 2,000. pensions can only be financed for expenditure that will not be incurred after the introduction of uniform pensions. Here I refer kindly to the calculation, which is in annex to the justification of the project on page. 14. The funds remaining under the pension and pension contributions will be sufficient to finance the remaining pensions and the surplus will remain in the budget. In addition, the resources collected in the OFE will be transferred to the budget, because that is the principle. Only these measures provide funding for this change for the first years. It should be emphasized with all power that for years there have been no systematic actions in Poland aimed at marginalising the grey zone in the field of the Customs Service activity, which we estimate today at more than 20 billion PLN; 20 billion PLN. The partial modernisation of the Customs Service carried out by the previous government and the solutions introduced with it do not fully restrict the functioning of the grey zone. One in three zlotys in the state budget comes from an economic activity supervised by the Customs Service. In 2014, customs officers seized almost 400 million cigarettes and more than 320 tons of tobacco, over 1 t and over 40 thousand. drugs, more than 670,000. And alcohol, over 7 million liters of fuel. They also occupied cultural goods, currencies, CITES specimens, drugs of unknown composition, amber, harmful toys and many, many other objects. They were eliminating illegal cigarette and alcohol factories, which you can unfortunately also find on the shelves in the store. On the other hand, losses of Polish tobacco companies are constantly increasing due to increased smuggling and illegal cigarette production. The loss of the spirit industry, the fuel industry, is increasing. The revenue of the state budget from excise duties is falling. I would like to quote you literally just a few typical disclosures made by Customs officers, e.g. 47 kg of cocaine at Chopin airport in 2012 or 2014 fresh disclosures at the transition in Dorohusk - 96 kg of heroin, one month later in Medyce 150 kg of heroin with a market value of PLN 30 million. On one of the premises, the customs officers revealed 14,000. I alcohol during the production of tinctures in which 15 times the permitted amount of methanol has been exceeded. Then, unfortunately, it also goes to the shelves in the shops. Nearly $7.5,000. And counterfeit alcohol in Łódź. Over 60,000. packets of cigarettes concreted in the empties on the transition to Medice. Customs e.g. In Chelm they thwarted the smuggling of nearly 2,000. stuffed animals. Customs keep about 5 million counterfeits a year, very often containing dangerous substances that pose a risk to the life and health of our children. Analyzing the conditions relating to the Customs Service, it is impossible to get the impression that the state, saving millions in pensions of customs officers, loses billions in terms of pathology in the management of the formation. This Act is one of the elements of the strengthening of the Customs Service, which, together with subsequent legal changes, is a prerequisite for a significant marginalisation of the grey zone and will allow the state budget to be provided with many billions of additional revenue, which will translate into a significant increase in the living rate of all its compatriots. In accordance with the Customs Service Act of 27 August 2009 in force today, this service is a uniform uniform form established to ensure the protection and security of the customs territory of the European Union, including the legality of imports into and exports of goods from that area, and the performance of the obligations laid down in the separate provisions, in particular as regards excise duty and gambling tax. These opinions are merely an example of a number of errors and decisions calling into question the Social Insurance Institution, contained in the draft law. However, the same Constitutional Tribunal in the same judgment indicates that this inequality concerns only parts of customs officers carrying out the so-called. police work that my previous speaker talked about in more detail. As we read in the Court's ruling, in so far as the persons entitled to pension are disregarding customs officers who carry out the tasks set out in Article. 2 (2) Point 1 (4) (6) of the Customs Service Act of 27 August 2009 is incompatible with Article. 32 constitutions. The Court referred to in paragraph 4.6 concerns only the task of identifying and combating crime and prosecuting crimes, not all the tasks performed by Customs officials. Modern fully understands that the law cannot treat people performing the same tasks or similar activities differently, but pension rights should be reduced rather than developed by further professional groups. Statistics on pension privileges are scary. For example, let's look at military pensions. In the 2015 budget, PLN 38 billion was earmarked for national defence. Almost 1/3 of this money is spent on salaries, and slightly less, about PLN 7 billion, on benefits, including over PLN 6 billion on pensions. Of these more than PLN 38 billion for technical modernisation of the Armed Forces is to be spent this year 8.2 billion PLN. The share of the burden of pension privileges is far too high. We spend too much on military wages and not enough on the military itself. In addition, in 2015 the deficit will amount to about PLN 60 billion. In this situation, giving pension privileges to one will make others have to work longer or receive lower pensions. The government wants to reverse the pension reform increasing retirement age. In such a situation, we will return to the old figures on the rise of demographic problems, which clearly show that within six years, between 2013 and 2019, the gap between workers and those who need to pay benefits will increase by 2.6 million people, as at that time 1.3 million people of working age will be lost and 1.3 million people of post-production age will arrive. The modern view is that the principle of justice and equality before the law requires that all workers, regardless of the place and nature of work, be subject to the same pension rules. We aim to eliminate separate pension systems for those starting a career, such as farmers, miners, policemen, and join the general pension system. Those already doing this work would, of course, maintain old pension rules for the protection of acquired rights. Modern supports the decision to raise the retirement age and will seek to maintain it. In an ageing society and a longer life, this is the only way to make pensions satisfactory. Lowering the retirement age will only mean lower pensions. The draft law still does not regulate the basic injustice of the privileges of selected professional groups giving rise to early retirement of selected groups or leading to their being subject to separate pension schemes. Therefore, in line with our promises made in the election campaign, we will put in place a law providing for the abolition of pension privileges for selected groups. [[Applause]] In conclusion, the proposed law aims to increase the privileges of professional groups, which is detrimental to society, to the sense of justice, to equality and to the detriment of the state budget. In view of the above, the Modern will vote against the bill. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2015-12-02-sejm-03-1.u290	Thank you, Marshal. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The draft law submitted by your President on the amendment of the Individual Income Tax Act aims to increase the tax-free amount. As you know, the tax-free amount should be both rational and fair, and above all it should safeguard the so-called. a minimum of existence, a minimum that satisfies the basic needs of man. At present, the tax-free amount is set out in the Individual Income Tax Act indirectly, i.e. by determining the amount to be deducted from the tax due. At the moment it amounts to PLN 556.02 which means that the amount which will not be subject to personal income tax is currently PLN 3091. This amount is very low, and the proposal submitted by the President proposes to increase this amount to 8 000. zł. This means that the amount which will be deducted from the tax due each year will be no longer PLN 556,02 but PLN 1440, which will mean a monthly deduction of the amount of PLN 120. According to data from the Institute of Labour and Social Affairs for last year, the amount that is needed to meet the basic needs, this minimum existence, is the amount of more than PLN 6500 per employee per year. For the pensioner's farm this amount is almost PLN 6200. Therefore, the current tax-free amount of PLN 3091 is almost twice as low. In his last judgment of 28 October this year, the Constitutional Tribunal pointed out that the tax-free amount should not be so low. This should be an amount that protects basic existential needs, which protects many people from falling into extreme poverty. The increase in the tax-free amount will primarily serve to safeguard against poverty, and will also enable many people who are currently earning very low income to meet these needs without having to benefit from the social security system. Individuals who receive an annual income of PLN 8000 and after applying the rounding provided for in the Tax Order - PLN 8002 will not pay any personal income tax at all. Those who exceed this amount slightly will pay the tax, but in a much lower amount. Such solutions are primarily intended to promote the elimination of social exclusion and are intended to safeguard, in particular, those who achieve the lowest income. They will undoubtedly have a very positive impact on retirement farms, as well as on households of ordinary low-income workers and those who are engaged in economic activities, but account for themselves on the basis of the Personal Income Tax Act. As far as this bill is concerned, it can be said that it opens the way to improving the existence of Poles. At the moment, in Poland the tax-free amount is one of the lowest in Europe. In Poland, this amount corresponds to approximately EUR 750. In Slovakia this is equivalent to EUR 3800. In Germany, this amount is much higher - more than EUR 8000. In this respect, the President's proposal responds to social expectations, especially those who are on the threshold of poverty. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

It's too bad Mr. President isn't going as far as we want him to go. Our voters who voted for us and our Members would like to abolish this unfair tax, which punishes people for their diligence, for entrepreneurship, which overcharges, together with social security contributions, human work, which often makes it uneconomical. In our view, the amount free of personal income tax and the amount free of compulsory social security contributions are the two most effective and best pro-family policy instruments. Pro-family policy is a priority for this government, which is why we would like these instruments to dominate its implementation. These are much better instruments than a widely advertised benefit - benefit 500+. We would like the amount free to be large enough to make sure that the great majority of Poles do not pay personal income tax or pay it symbolically. We would like it to be like in Hungary, where families with three children in practice do not pay income tax, and families with two children pay significantly less tax than would have been the case on the tax scale. On behalf of the Kukiz Poselski Club15, I would like to make an amendment to the bill if it is appropriate to amend it in committee. There in the committee we will propose an amendment so that the amount is free - we have these three demands - firstly, it was bigger, secondly, to think about ensuring its growth in the following years, so it would not be so that this year it increases once again, and then we will wait again 10 years with the same amount. We would like to introduce a mechanism for increasing this amount gradually so that this amount is ultimately equivalent to the annual minimum wage. Today it is approx. 23.24 thousand. zł. That would be the amount -- tax-free amount -- equal to the minimum wage. In our view, this would be an appropriate target amount. Third request. We would like the tax-free amount to be, as in the case of its operation in the UK, an intelligently progressive amount, i.e. to those who exceed the corresponding income - in our case 85 528 PLN, i.e. one where 32 percent of the income tax rate is paid - to those who earn above, gradually reduced the tax-free amount. We propose that for excess income above PLN 85 528 for a zloty, the amount free of charge should be reduced by 50 gr, i.e. for 2 PLN - 1 PLN respectively, so that when the income is obtained, the persons who exceed such income do not have a tax-free amount. This mechanism would, in our view, increase the tax-free amount to 10,000 today. zł, not, as you suggest the president - up to 8,000. zł. In our view, the statutory changes will, of course, result in, if there is no compensation, a reduction in the revenue of local governments, but in our view the best interests of citizens, the welfare of taxpayers rather than the welfare of civil servants are more important. The last 8 years of the Civic Platform and PSL governments have been an increase in civil servants' incomes and a decline in citizens' incomes. It is high time to reverse this trend so that the disposable income of citizens increases and the disposable income of officials decreases, because that is what the state of freedom is about, that citizens decide about their money, not officials. The Kukiz Club will, of course, recommend the transfer of the project to the committees and, as I mentioned, we will actively act there and submit our amendments, in particular with regard to the amendment on the free degressive amount. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2015-12-02-sejm-03-1.u298	Mrs. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, The primary objective of the amendment proposed by your President to the Act on Individual Income Tax is, as we read, to improve the economic situation of households. The way to achieve this is to increase the tax-free amount from PLN 3091 to PLN 8,000. zł. Modern, of course, is a proponent of reducing the tax burden on households. We assume that a citizen can spend money better than you can. However, there are several aspects of this law that concern us. Unfortunately, the new amount of free money will not improve the economic situation of the poorest. And this, let me remind you, was the main intention of the Constitutional Tribunal ruling in this case. As stated in the explanatory memorandum, in 2013 as much as 16% the total payers received income not exceeding 8 000. £ per year. Paradoxically, the 16% the least earning Poles practically from the increased amount of free will not benefit. In the case of this group of persons, the higher amount free will instead replace the deduction from the health insurance tax. I will remind you that health insurance of 7,75% We deduct from the tax due. So if the taxpayer does not earn more than 8,000 in a given year. zł, will not have a tax due and will not be able to deduct the paid health insurance contribution. One copy will therefore replace the other, without any added value for that taxpayer. The second problem we see is the proceeds disappearing from the local government money. According to our calculations, the increase in the amount of free will reduce PIT revenue by up to PLN 20 billion. But even if we accepted the applicant's calculations, PIT revenues would decrease by PLN 15.5 billion per year. Let us remind you that more than half of the revenue from PIT feeds the cash registers of local governments at different levels. This means for them a loss of revenue of about PLN 8 billion. The project presented does not solve this problem. The vision of the return of these funds to self-government by stimulating consumption and higher profits of companies does not have a hard economic basis, but rather resembles a wishful thinking. However, such a loss in the income of local governments could mean another closed school, broken roads and the growing debt of the poorest municipalities. At the previous meeting of the Sejm, a citizens' draft law called for compensation to local governments for the decline in income resulting from previous tax concessions was read. This amount was calculated at that time for about PLN 12 billion per year. Now we want to take another 8 billion from local governments. The idea is important, the goal is absolutely right, but it is time to think about how to compensate local governments for these disappearing revenues. Of course, we may not see public debt rising, we can leave local governments to themselves, but this means reducing the quality of life in our cities, municipalities and counties. Further decisions and actions are therefore needed. We see three possible ways to solve this problem. Firstly, the increase in the share of local authorities in PIT, secondly, the transfer of part of the VAT revenue to local authorities, thirdly, the increase in the general subsidy. Therefore, the modern club recommends that the amendment of this law in committees be further reviewed together with the amendment of the law on the income of local government. [[♪ The bell ♪]] In conclusion, the modern project will support an increase in tax-free amount, it is important that Poles earn more. However, we see in this act a threat to local governments. We are happy to take part in the work that solves these problems, and we have concrete proposals in this area. Let us also bear in mind that we must not increase the state's deficit as we lower the state's income. In return, it is worth thinking about savings - a more modest administration, a reduction in privileges, a greater economy of the state and its representatives. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2015-12-02-sejm-03-1.u299	Thank you, Member. I ask Mrs Genowefa Tokarska to speak on behalf of the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2015-12-02-sejm-03-1.u300	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party, I will present my position on the draft law according to print no. 51. The bill, as has been said, concerns an increase in the amount of personal income tax free. It increases this tax-free amount from 3,091 thousand. PLN up to 8,000. zł. This change is introduced in the project, as has been the case so far, not directly, but through an amount reducing the tax due. In the current law, the amount by which tax is reduced is PLN 556.02 and the amendment proposes to increase it to PLN 1,440 thousand. zł. By analogy, the taxable person who receives an annual income of 8 000 will not be obliged to pay the tax, and even in the light of the rules of rounding, this amount exempt from tax will amount to 8,002 thousand. zł. It is proposed that the Act enter into force on 1 January 2016. Thus, taking into account the rules on the settlement of this tax, payers, i.e. workers, will be required to calculate and collect in the course of the year advances on the income tax of persons liable to pay this tax. The amount of the advance will therefore be reduced by 1/12 of the tax reduction amount. Therefore, today's reduction, which amounts to PLN 46,33, will increase to PLN 120. The proposed regulation aims to improve the economic situation of households and businesses subject to tax on their income tax on individuals. By setting an amount exempt from tax of 8 000. zł, the designer established it slightly above the so-called. a minimum of existence, which is slightly over 6.5 thousand respectively. for a single employee holding and slightly above 6,1 thousand. PLN, almost 6.2 thousand, for a single-person pensioner. The amount of non-taxation so determined will result in people whose income is low being not liable to pay the tax. I would like to stress that this proposal also responds to the assessment of the situation raised by the Constitutional Tribunal in its judgment of 28 October 2015. The current amount of non-taxation, which must be acknowledged, is grossly inadequate in relation to socio-economic realities, as the tax also charges the income of those whose height does not even ensure the biological existence of human beings. In that judgment, the Constitutional Tribunal held that Article. Article 27 (2) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 In so far as it does not provide for a mechanism to correct the amount reducing the tax guaranteeing at least the minimum existence, it is incompatible with Article. Article 2 and Article 2 84 of the Constitution and, therefore, the Court has ruled that it is losing its power to the extent indicated on 30 November next year. However, the draft law, which must be emphasised in particular, will have a significant impact on public finances, the revenue of the state budget and the revenue of local government units. Although the designer emphasizes the so-called. the multiplier effect by increasing consumer demand, but the question arises as to whether it is overly optimistic. It is true that some of the lost budget revenue will return to the public finance sector, but the projector assumes that a significant part of the untaxed amount will be used for consumption purposes. I hope that the financial consequences of these introductions must be acknowledged, [[♪ The bell ♪]] good changes will be reliably calculated by the finance department of the current government, and the government will also provide, with particular emphasis, compensation for the lost income of local government units. My Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party is in favour of transferring the project to further work in Parliament. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2015-12-02-sejm-03-1.u381	Mr. Marshal! Mr President, thank you for this declaration. The current situation on the pig market is the result of several factors. Since the beginning of 2014, when African swine fever appeared in the European Union and restrictions on imports by the Russian Federation have been introduced, difficulties have arisen with the disposal of parts of pigmeat produced in the European Union. A 2% increase in pork production in the European Union has been imposed on this. , which has contributed to an increase in surplus with the loss of part of the markets. The Polish pigmeat market is strongly linked to the European market. Buying-in prices are also falling in the European Union, although they fall to a lesser extent than in Poland. The pork market in Poland is also affected by problems that have grown for many years, unfortunately, the PO-PSL government has done little in this area for eight years. Many export markets, particularly Asian ones, are also difficult to access due to the consequences of African swine fever. The Ministry of Agriculture therefore takes ad hoc action and long-term action to improve the current situation. Minister Krzysztof Jurgel sent Commissioner Hogan a letter in which he raised the issue of the difficult situation on the pigmeat market and asked for action to stabilise it. The European Commission has decided to mobilise aid for private storage of pigmeat since 4 January 2016, but Poland requests that these measures be accelerated. In addition, we have drawn attention to the disposal of meat collected as a result of private storage aid. This should take place on third markets outside the European Union market in order to permanently remove this surplus from the Union market. To this end, it is necessary to activate export refunds for pigmeat. In addition, a request was made to discuss the deteriorating situation on the pigmeat market at the European Union Council meeting on Agriculture and Fisheries to be held on 14 and 15 December. Minister Jurgiel is going to this meeting. We want to draw the attention of other Member States to the need for rapid action, as well as to the analysis of the needs of the sector in the long term. I would also like to inform you that in connection with the signals that meat processing establishments apply low buying-in prices to pigs from domestic producers, which suggests monopolistic practices and collusion of prices, the Minister of Agriculture and Rural Development asked the Office for Competition and Consumer Protection to examine whether the market behaviour of the pig-buyers is not in breach of the competition rules. In particular, we asked to examine whether there are frauds in the accounting of the overlay system by plants and whether plants pay more for raw materials imported from abroad in relation to prices offered to Polish producers and thus do not affect the conditions of competition. The relevant inspection services are also to verify information about the poorer quality of the imported raw material. In addition, representatives of the Minister for Agriculture and Rural Development and subordinate services met with representatives of farmers, agricultural organisations, as well as representatives of slaughterhouses and processing plants to discuss the causes and consequences of the current situation on the pig market. The Ministry is also preparing a long-term action programme to stabilise the situation in the pigmeat sector. These activities will include increased surveillance of the quality of products of animal origin, as well as speeding up the discussions on access to and removal of veterinary and sanitary barriers to pigmeat in those markets. We will work to improve the effectiveness of promotion funds for the promotion of Polish pigmeat. It is also important to minimise the effects of ASF on pig production. In addition, we are working on a programme to rebuild pig production. The main objectives of this programme have already been developed. As for the third week of Minister Jurgel's work, this is already a significant part of the work that we have been able to prepare for the development of breeding flocks, the purchase of genetic material, the simplification of the rules for obtaining support under the rural programme, the creation of a guarantee fund for slaughterhouses and factories with the participation of capital farmers. It will also be important to improve the quality of advice for farmers and to set up an insurance system and a fund to compensate farmers for losses, including pig farming. Poland also wants to be actively involved in the work related to the review of the common agricultural policy by preparing proposals for changes in the pigmeat market. We also have further meetings planned with producers and processors. [[♪ The bell ♪]] We are counting on a substantive discussion in this regard. The pig market is at the moment a priority for the Minister of Agriculture and Rural Development. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2015-12-02-sejm-03-1.u388	Thank you very much. The State Secretary at the Ministry of Internal Affairs and Administration, Mr Jakub Skiba, answers the question. You're welcome, Minister.
ParlaMint-PL_2015-12-02-sejm-03-1.u389	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, I would like to inform you that the preliminary assessment carried out in 2013 and 2014 by the previous government of the reorganisation of the Border Guard is currently being carried out. The Ministry of Internal Affairs and Administration analyses the facts in Border Guard units and the functioning of structures, with particular regard to the changes made. It should be stressed that for the government of the Republic of Poland the security of citizens is the highest priority and the issue of security is therefore crucial, in particular in the context of the current situation related to the migration crisis, the recent attacks in Paris and the terrorist threat occurring also outside the European Union. All necessary actions in the area of border security will therefore be undertaken taking full account of security considerations. Here we see a clear connection. Recent developments and dynamic changes make it necessary to review the current structure of the Border Guard throughout the country and adapt it to new challenges. Therefore, the Ministry of Internal Affairs and Administration is currently assessing and verifying the solutions adopted, bearing in mind those objectives which I have been talking about. One of the changes made by the previous government was the abolition of the Carpathian Border Guard Division. This change consisted in deforming the command of the branch, while the Border Guard facilities after the former branch, after the branch that was liquidated, were included in the territorial scope of the Silesia-Małopolska Border Guard Division. As a result of these changes, the Carpathian Border Guard Support Centre was established in Nowy Sącz. This branch is intended to support the activities carried out by the other units and the tasks commissioned by the Chief Border Guard. As a result of the liquidation of the Carpathian Border Guard Division, 673 officers were transferred to serve in other units, 651 of which were ex officio transfers, and 22 were transfers at the request of the officers themselves. It is also worth noting that, until 2010, three investments were made at the headquarters of the Karpackie Border Guard Division, which resulted in modernization of the premises in which the headquarters of the branch headquarters were located. These objects are currently only partially used by the Border Guard, i.e. according to their original purpose. I would like to assure you, ladies and gentlemen, that following the overarching priority of the security of our country's citizens, the Ministry of Home Affairs and Administration will decide on the necessary changes to the current organizational structures of the Border Guard. Strengthening the Border Guard in the current situation is an overarching action for the government. We will therefore strengthen the external border of Poland, which is also the external border of the European Union and the Schengen area. However, we also see the need to strengthen the protection of the internal borders of the Schengen area, especially in the southern direction from which we can expect the influx of immigrants due to the current migration crisis. The migration crisis is linked to security issues, terrorist threats and the destabilisation of the Schengen area due to the ineffectiveness of the existing border security systems. This does not apply to Poland, it is a pan-European problem. So far, this situation has not been about Poland, which effectively defends borders and which is basically outside the main routes of immigration, but this can change radically and suddenly, because the development of the migration crisis is, please, unpredictable. This means that the Polish Border Guard is to a large extent responsible for the stability of the whole Schengen area and should be worked on its organisational, human and technical reinforcement. In this context, decisions concerning the liquidation of the Carpathian Border Guard Division do not seem appropriate given its geographical location and the potential increase in attempts to cross the border illegally due to the migration crisis. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, The organisational changes should serve as effectively as possible to carry out the tasks assigned to the Border Guard. Therefore, it is necessary to carry out a detailed analysis concerning: the possibility of the occurrence of risks, especially from the southern border of the Republic of Poland, the state of the Border Guard infrastructure at the entire internal border, as well as the economic costs of possible organisational changes and the location of the premises. I would add that not possible, but very likely organisational changes. Therefore, decisions on the restoration of the deformed facilities and the Karpackie Border Guard Branch, to be taken, will be preceded by a thorough verification of the risks, location and costs. The Ministry of Internal Affairs and Administration will take appropriate decisions on this matter as soon as possible. The changes are likely to be made next year. Thank you very much.

Will the results of the studies evaluating the highly qualified and skilled six-year-olds who took up the school responsibility and the very high results of Polish junior high school students in European competence tests be taken into account in the implementation of the changes?
ParlaMint-PL_2015-12-02-sejm-03-1.u488	Statement on the 104th anniversary of Gracian Frog. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! 8 December this year We will celebrate the 104th anniversary of the birth of Gracian Froga ps. Szczerniec. I would therefore like to remind you of the character of this enchanted soldier, captain of infantry and defending the armored Polish Army, commander of 3. The Vilnius AK Brigade. Gracian Frog was born on December 8, 1911 in Lasovka, Podkarpacie. He was the son of Stanisław and Bronisława from Pawłowska's house. After his graduation, he joined the Podchożych Piechota School in Komorów-Ostrowa Mazowiecka. He graduated at the age of 22 as a lieutenant. He served in 2. The Podhale Rifle Regiment in Sanok, and in the following years in 4. Armoured battalion in Brest on Bug and 7. An armored battalion in Grodno. After the outbreak of World War II, Gracian Frog participated in the September campaign as commander of the Technical and Economic Squadron in 33. Armored Division operating within the Vilnius Cavalry Brigade. He fought in the battle of Tomaszow Lubelski. At that time, he was captured by a German slave, but he managed to escape from it. He reached Przemyśl, where the Soviets arrested him. This is the second time he's managed to escape. Gracian Frog returned to Vilnius, where he worked as a conspiracy in the regiment circles. After the arrest by the NKVD in 1941, he avoided moving into the USSR thanks to Polish railwaymen who unhooked the last few wagons with prisoners. After Vilnius was under German occupation, Frog began conspiracy activities in the ranks of the National Army. He commanded a guerrilla unit in the Sugan area, which in time began to be called 3. The AK brigade. This branch took part in many armed actions against Germany and the collaborating Lithuanian auxiliary police. As a result of their conduct, weapons, ammunition, and equipment were acquired. From 13 to 14 May 1944 near the village of Murowa Oświadka 3. The Vilnius Brigade fought a battle with the Lithuanian Local Corps. It ended with the victory of Poles and the breakdown of the collaborative formation. Gracian Frog, along with his brigade, also took part in the fights for the Vilnius fought as part of Operation Ostra Gate. On 14 July 1944, Szczerbiec was promoted to the rank of captain. A few days after the promotion, Gracian Frog was arrested by the NKVD. He was sent to prison in Lukishki, Vilnius, and then transferred to the NKVD camp in Diagilev near Rizania in the Soviet Union. After nearly 2 years he managed to escape from the camp and reach Vilnius and then to Łódź. In July 1948, in the framework of the X-Action, he was arrested by the Security Office officers. He was accused of conspiracy activities within the Vilnius District Mobilization Centre of the AK. He was also accused of collaborating with Germany in the fight against the Soviet guerrilla in Vilnius. In November 1950, Gracian Frog was sentenced to death. The sentence was executed in May of the following year in the Mokotów prison. Szczerbiec was rehabilitated in 1959. His symbolic grave is found at the Military Cemetery in Powązki, Warsaw, in a building on the Link. Among the awards given to him are the Order of Virtuti Militari (given twice, once postmortem), the Silver Cross of Merit with Swords and the Cross of Valiant. Hello and praise the heroes! Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2015-12-02-sejm-03-1.u489	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The chanting of the word'solidarity' by Members of the Civic Platform to defend their political interests is an insult to trade unionists with 'Solidarity'. For 8 years, the governments of the Civic Platform have destroyed social dialogue in Poland, mocked workers' and trade union rights, and today they are trying to enter into the rights of members of the union. The name and character of the NSZZ (Solidarity) are legally reserved. The decision to use them is decided by the National Commission of the NSZZ (Solidarity) and I suspect that no one has addressed it in this matter. I would like to point out to the young Members of Modern America that we were the ones who paid with freedom and health without agreeing to the laws of martial law to live in a democratic country. Ladies, you take advantage of the effects of our business without any cost. Meanwhile, you are boldly humiliating those who have incurred these costs. I would like to ask you to think a little bit about the latest history of Poland. As for the Constitutional Tribunal, I would like to remind you that 14 out of 15 judges were elected at the request of the Civic Platform. Selected at the request of Law and Justice - 0. This determines the nature of this attack. Thank you.
